========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I still like the *idea* of it, I think. I mean, I don't want to sound ungrateful for my skills or my shop. It's a good trade. I should be happy doing it. 

But, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope this doesn't sound terrible—it just doesn't bring me that same... spark? I used to love the smell of the sawdust and figuring out a tricky joint, but now it's just... work. Just motions I go through. I try to put a good face on it, especially around the clients, because you don't want to hire a miserable carpenter, right? 

I don't know, is it normal to just sort of... lose the taste for things you've always liked? I feel like I'm letting myself down by not enjoying it more.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I really try to be social when I can. I know it's good for me. But lately, I guess I've been pulling back a bit. I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything. 

It's just... I've stopped going to things I used to enjoy, like pickup basketball or gallery openings for some of the local artists I know. I just haven't had the energy for it. And, honestly, it's starting to bleed into my work, too, which I feel terrible about. I've missed some bids and lost a couple of clients who used to call me first for jobs. I even let some mortgage paperwork pile up because I just... I can't seem to keep up with the basic parts of running my business right now. 

I feel like I'm letting everyone down, including myself. I'm barely keeping a foothold with the people and the work that matter to me. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be letting things slip like this. Is it normal for things to get this hard to manage? I really want to do better, I promise.

Well, I... I try really hard not to let that happen. I know I need to stay active, especially with the carpentry work. If I stop caring, the business suffers, and I really don't want to let anyone down.

I guess... maybe I don't get quite as excited about starting a new project as I used to. Like, I still do the work, and I make sure it's done right, I promise. I'm not just sitting around doing nothing. But that feeling of, you know, really looking forward to getting into the shop and working with the wood... it's just a little harder to find lately. 

I still force myself to go out and do things, though. I don't want to be one of those guys who just gives up on everything. I'm still trying my best to stay engaged. Is that... is it normal to just have to push yourself a little harder sometimes? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy.

Well, um... I guess it's been a while now. I really try not to dwell on it, you know? I want to stay positive. But, if I'm being completely honest... it started around the time I took on the mortgage for the new studio space.

It's more like I just wake up and try to get through the day as best I can. \n\nI'm sorry, that's probably not what you want to hear, is it? I feel like I'm just giving you all these depressing answers, and I hope I'm not being too frustrating to work with. Is it... is it okay that I can't really think of anything right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you been wanting to be around other people lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be social when I can. I know it's good for me. But lately, I guess I've been pulling back a bit. I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything. \n\nIt's just... I've stopped going to things I used to enjoy, like pickup basketball or gallery openings for some of the local artists I know. I just haven't had the energy for it. And, honestly, it's starting to bleed into my work, too, which I feel terrible about. I've missed some bids and lost a couple of clients who used to call me first for jobs. I even let some mortgage paperwork pile up because I just... I can't seem to keep up with the basic parts of running my business right now. \n\nI feel like I'm letting everyone down, including myself. I'm barely keeping a foothold with the people and the work that matter to me. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be letting things slip like this. Is it normal for things to get this hard to manage? I really want to do better, I promise.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I've really been trying to push through it, you know? I didn't want to make a big fuss. But... I guess it's been a while now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time I took on the mortgage for my new studio space. That was... gosh, a little over two years ago? Maybe two years and two months, if I'm being exact. \n\nI really thought I'd bounce back by now, I honestly did. I kept telling myself it was just the stress of the new financial commitment and that I'd get used to it. I'm sorry it's gone on this long without me getting a handle on it. Well, I... I try really hard not to let it take over my whole week, you know? I want to be productive. But, um... I guess if I'm being completely honest, it's most days.

I've stopped going to things I used to enjoy, like pickup basketball or gallery openings for some of the local artists I know. I just haven't had the energy for it. And I still walk my dog, Buster, because he needs it, but it feels more like a chore I have to get through than something fun. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds awful, like I'm just this big wet blanket. It's more like I just wake up and try to get through the day as best I can. \n\nI'm sorry, that's probably not what you want to hear, is it? I feel like I'm just giving you all these depressing answers, and I hope I'm not being too frustrating to work with. I'm barely keeping a foothold with the people and the work that matter to me. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be letting things slip like this. Is it normal for things to get this hard to manage? I really want to do better, I promise.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I've really been trying to push through it, you know? I didn't want to make a big fuss. But... I guess it's been a while now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time I took on the mortgage for my new studio space. That was... gosh, a little over two years ago? Maybe two years and two months, if I'm being exact. \n\nI really thought I'd bounce back by now, I honestly did. I kept telling myself it was just the stress of the new financial commitment and that I'd get used to it. I'm sorry it's gone on this long without me getting a handle on it. I know I should have figured out how to cope better by now. Is it... is it bad that it's been over two years? I hope I haven't let it go too far to be fixed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, nothing like that, I promise. I know... I know I've been saying how hard things are, and I hope I haven't given you the wrong impression. But actually wanting to end things? No, I wouldn't do that. I know that would just cause more trouble for everyone else, and I really don't want to be a burden like that. \n\nI hope that's the right answer... I mean, it's the truth, I just... I don't want you to think I'm giving up entirely. I really want to give you a good answer here. \n\nI guess... well, no, not really. I mean, I watch TV sometimes in the evenings, but it's more just to have noise in the house, you know? Not because I'm really getting a kick out of whatever's on. And I still walk my dog, Buster, because he needs it, but it feels more like a chore I have to get through than something fun. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds awful, like I'm just this big wet blanket. I really try to find the bright side of things, I promise I do. It's just... everything feels kind of gray right now. Is that okay? I mean, is it helpful for me to tell you that, or am I just being too negative?\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still look forward to?\n\nPatient: I really want to say yes, I really do. I feel like I'm just giving you all these depressing answers, and I hope I'm not being too frustrating to work with. Is it... is it okay that I can't really think of anything right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you been wanting to be around other people lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be social when I can. I know it's good for me. But lately, I guess I've been pulling back a bit. I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything. \n\nIt's just... I've stopped going to things I used to enjoy, like pickup basketball or gallery openings for some of the local artists I know. I just haven't had the energy for it. And, honestly, it's starting to bleed into my work, too, which I feel terrible about. I've missed some bids and lost a couple of clients who used to call me first for jobs. I even let some mortgage paperwork pile up because I just... I can't seem to keep up with the basic parts of running my business right now. \n\nI feel like I'm letting everyone down, including myself. I'm barely keeping a foothold with the people and the work that matter to me. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be letting things slip like this. Is it normal for things to get this hard to manage? I really want to do better, I promise.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I've really been trying to push through it, you know? I didn't want to make a big fuss. But... I guess it's been a while now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time I took on the mortgage for my new studio space. That was... gosh, a little over two years ago? Maybe two years and two months, if I'm being exact. \n\nI really thought I'd bounce back by now, I honestly did. I kept telling myself it was just the stress of the new financial commitment and that I'd get used to it. I'm sorry it's gone on this long without me getting a handle on it. But actually wanting to end things? No, I wouldn't do that. I know that would just cause more trouble for everyone else, and I really don't want to be a burden like that. \n\nI hope that's the right answer... I mean, it's the truth, I just... I don't want you to think I'm giving up entirely. Is it okay that I just feel tired, as long as I'm not having those kinds of thoughts?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to eat right and get enough rest, I promise I do. I know how important it is to take care of myself. But, honestly, I haven't really noticed any big changes. \n\nI mean, I eat when I can, and I sleep... well, I sleep about the same as I usually do, I think. I haven't been keeping a strict log or anything, but nothing stands out as being really different or, you know, wrong. \n\nI hope that's okay. I know sometimes people stop eating or sleep all day when they feel like this, but I'm really trying to keep my routines going as best I can. Does that sound normal to you? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to help myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I hope I haven't let it go too far to be fixed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, nothing like that, I promise. I know... I know I've been saying how hard things are, and I hope I haven't given you the wrong impression. I really do want to get better, and I want to be here to fix things with my business and my life. \n\nI mean, sometimes I just wish I could sleep for a really long time, just to get a break from feeling so tired and overwhelmed. But actually wanting to end things? No, I wouldn't do that. I know that would just cause more trouble for everyone else, and I really don't want to be a burden like that. \n\nI hope that's the right answer... I mean, it's the truth, I just... I don't want you to think I'm giving up entirely. Is it okay that I just feel tired, as long as I'm not having those kinds of thoughts?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to eat right and get enough rest, I promise I do. I know how important it is to take care of myself. I haven't been keeping a strict log or anything, but nothing stands out as being really different or, you know, wrong. \n\nI hope that's okay. I know sometimes people stop eating or sleep all day when they feel like this, but I'm really trying to keep my routines going as best I can. Does that sound normal to you? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to help myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try really hard not to let that happen. I know I need to stay active, especially with the carpentry work. If I stop caring, the business suffers, and I really don't want to let anyone down.\n\nI guess... maybe I don't get quite as excited about starting a new project as I used to. Like, I still do the work, and I make sure it's done right, I promise. I'm not just sitting around doing nothing. But that feeling of, you know, really looking forward to getting into the shop and working with the wood... it's just a little harder to find lately. \n\nI still force myself to go out and do things, though. I don't want to be one of those guys who just gives up on everything. I'm still trying my best to stay engaged. Is that... is it normal to just have to push yourself a little harder sometimes? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just being lazy.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like you've lost interest in the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it's been a while now. I really try not to dwell on it, you know? I want to stay positive. I just... I thought I was just stressed about the financial part of it, you know? Taking on a big responsibility like that. And it's silly, because I have a good job, you know? But even when I'm in the shop, surrounded by the wood and the tools I've always loved... it all just feels so flat. Sometimes I have to really fight with myself just to keep working, instead of just standing there staring at the wall. \n\nI feel bad even complaining about it, honestly. Does that... does that sound like the kind of thing you usually hear from people? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy the things you used to like, such as working with wood?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I still like the *idea* of it, I think. I mean, I don't want to sound ungrateful for my skills or my shop. It's a good trade. I should be happy doing it. \n\nBut, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope this doesn't sound terrible—it just doesn't bring me that same... spark? I used to love the smell of the sawdust and figuring out a tricky joint, but now it's just... work. Just motions I go through. I try to put a good face on it, especially around the clients, because you don't want to hire a miserable carpenter, right? \n\nI don't know, is it normal to just sort of... lose the taste for things you've always liked? I feel like I'm letting myself down by not enjoying it more.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm trying to think. I really want to give you a good answer here. \n\nI guess... well, no, not really. I mean, I watch TV sometimes in the evenings, but it's more just to have noise in the house, you know? Not because I'm really getting a kick out of whatever's on. And I still walk my dog, Buster, because he needs it, but it feels more like a chore I have to get through than something fun. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds awful, like I'm just this big wet blanket. I really try to find the bright side of things, I promise I do. It's just... everything feels kind of gray right now. Is that okay? I mean, is it helpful for me to tell you that, or am I just being too negative?\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still look forward to?\n\nPatient: I really want to say yes, I really do. I know I should be appreciating the little things... like a good cup of coffee in the morning, or, you know, a nice afternoon. I try to notice them, I promise. \n\nBut... actually looking forward to them? I don't think I really... I just don't feel that anticipation right now. It's more like I just wake up and try to get through the day as best I can. \n\nI'm sorry, that's probably not what you want to hear, is it? I feel like I'm just giving you all these depressing answers, and I hope I'm not being too frustrating to work with. Is it... is it okay that I can't really think of anything right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you been wanting to be around other people lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be social when I can. I know it's good for me. But lately, I guess I've been pulling back a bit. I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything. \n\nIt's just... I've stopped going to things I used to enjoy, like pickup basketball or gallery openings for some of the local artists I know. I just haven't had the energy for it. And, honestly, it's starting to bleed into my work, too, which I feel terrible about. I've missed some bids and lost a couple of clients who used to call me first for jobs. I even let some mortgage paperwork pile up because I just... I can't seem to keep up with the basic parts of running my business right now. \n\nI feel like I'm letting everyone down, including myself. I'm barely keeping a foothold with the people and the work that matter to me. I'm sorry, I know I shouldn't be letting things slip like this. Is it normal for things to get this hard to manage? I really want to do better, I promise.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I've really been trying to push through it, you know? I didn't want to make a big fuss. But... I guess it's been a while now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time I took on the mortgage for my new studio space. That was... gosh, a little over two years ago? Maybe two years and two months, if I'm being exact. \n\nI really thought I'd bounce back by now, I honestly did. I kept telling myself it was just the stress of the new financial commitment and that I'd get used to it. I'm sorry it's gone on this long without me getting a handle on it. I guess I came in because I've just been struggling a bit lately. \n\nI'm a carpenter, and I've always prided myself on being a hard worker, you know? I try to do the right thing. But lately, I honestly just can't see my studio—or, well, my life really—getting back on track anymore. I hate to sound like a complainer, I really do, but no matter how hard I work or what kind of payment plan the bank puts in front of me, it just feels like a dead end. \n\nI hope it's okay that I came to you for this. Does that make sense, feeling stuck like that?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I mean, I wouldn't want to overstate it or make it sound worse than it is... I know everyone gets down sometimes, right? But... yeah, I guess \"sad\" or \"empty\" is pretty close. \n\nMost days, I wake up and there's just this... this heavy, gray feeling already sitting on my chest before I even get out of bed. And it's silly, because I have a good job, you know? But even when I'm in the shop, surrounded by the wood and the tools I've always loved... it all just feels so flat. Sometimes I have to really fight with myself just to keep working, instead of just standing there staring at the wall. \n\nI feel bad even complaining about it, honestly. Does that... does that sound like the kind of thing you usually hear from people? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're still able to enjoy the things you used to like, such as working with wood?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I still like the *idea* of it, I think. I mean, I don't want to sound ungrateful for my skills or my shop. It's a good trade. I should be happy doing it. \n\nBut, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope this doesn't sound terrible—it just doesn't bring me that same... spark? I used to love the smell of the sawdust and figuring out a tricky joint, but now it's just... work. Just motions I go through. I try to put a good face on it, especially around the clients, because you don't want to hire a miserable carpenter, right? \n\nI don't know, is it normal to just sort of... lose the taste for things you've always liked? I feel like I'm letting myself down by not enjoying it more.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm trying to think. I really want to give you a good answer here. \n\nI guess... well, no, not really. I mean, I watch TV sometimes in the evenings, but it's more just to have noise in the house, you know? Not because I'm really getting a kick out of whatever's on. And I still walk my dog, Buster, because he needs it, but it feels more like a chore I have to get through than something fun. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds awful, like I'm just this big wet blanket. I really try to find the bright side of things, I promise I do. It's just... everything feels kind of gray right now. Is that okay? I mean, is it helpful for me to tell you that, or am I just being too negative?\n\nDoctor: Are there any small pleasures in life that you still look forward to?\n\nPatient: I really want to say yes, I really do. I know I should be appreciating the little things... like a good cup of coffee in the morning, or, you know, a nice afternoon. I try to notice them, I promise. \n\nBut... actually looking forward to them? I don't think I really... I just don't feel that anticipation right now. It's more like I just wake up and try to get through the day as best I can. \n\nI'm sorry, that's probably not what you want to hear, is it? I feel like I'm just giving you all these depressing answers, and I hope I'm not being too frustrating to work with. Is it... is it okay that I can't really think of anything right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you been wanting to be around other people lately?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I really try to be social when I can. I know it's good for me. But lately, I guess I've been pulling back a bit. I hope that doesn't make me sound antisocial or anything. \n\nIt's just... I've stopped going to things I used to enjoy, like pickup basketball or gallery openings for some of the local artists I know. I just haven't had the energy for it. I really want to do better, I promise.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I've really been trying to push through it, you know? I didn't want to make a big fuss. But... I guess it's been a while now. \n\nIt kind of started around the time I took on the mortgage for my new studio space. That was... gosh, a little over two years ago? Maybe two years and two months, if I'm being exact. \n\nI really thought I'd bounce back by now, I honestly did. I kept telling myself it was just the stress of the new financial commitment and that I'd get used to it. I'm sorry it's gone on this long without me getting a handle on it. I know I should have figured out how to cope better by now. Is it... is it bad that it's been over two years? I hope I haven't let it go too far to be fixed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, nothing like that, I promise. I know... I haven't been keeping a strict log or anything, but nothing stands out as being really different or, you know, wrong. \n\nI hope that's okay. I know sometimes people stop eating or sleep all day when they feel like this, but I'm really trying to keep my routines going as best I can. Does that sound normal to you? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to help myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in most activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I try really hard not to let that happen. I know I need to stay active, especially with the carpentry work. If I stop caring, the business suffers, and I really don't want to let anyone down.\n\nI guess... maybe I don't get quite as excited about starting a new project as I used to. Like, I still do the work, and I make sure it's done right, I promise. I'm not just sitting around doing nothing. But that feeling of, you know, really looking forward to getting into the shop and working with the wood... it's just a little harder to find lately. \n\nI still force myself to go out and do things, though. I don't want to be one of those guys who just gives up on everything. I'm still trying my best to stay engaged. Is that... is it normal to just have to push yourself a little harder sometimes?
